Hi, thank you so much for seeing me. I really appreciate it. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time, it's probably nothing too major, just a bit of a bad habit, I guess. 

But what finally pushed me to come in is... well, I keep getting up during things where I really know I should just stay put. Like, during sprint planning meetings for work, or even recently when my husband and I were on these important mortgage calls. I know I need to just sit there and focus, but I'll suddenly feel like I absolutely need to go get a glass of water, or I'll decide I have to check something in the other room right that second. 

I'm really not trying to be rude or disrespectful to whoever is talking! It's just a little quirk I've been struggling to manage. I hope that makes sense? Is that the sort of thing I should be talking to you about?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... it's a little hard to explain. I hope I don't sound too strange saying this. It's not really a specific thought, like I'm actively deciding to leave. It's more of a... I guess a physical feeling? 

Like, even if I'm sitting there looking perfectly calm on the outside—and I really am trying my absolute best to be polite and pay attention—there's just this weird pressure building up inside me. It almost feels like I just absolutely *have* to move, or switch to a different task, or at least do something with my hands. If I don't, I get this silly feeling like I'm going to just crawl out of my skin. 

I know that sounds a bit dramatic, I'm sorry! It's probably just normal nervous energy, right? Am I explaining that okay?

Um, well... I don't think I make a lot of careless mistakes, I really try to be very thorough with my work. But, um, maybe the focus part a little bit? It's nothing too terrible, I promise.

Um... organize things? Well, I try to keep track of everything, I really do! I know how important it is to stay on top of things, especially at work. 

I guess... maybe sometimes my system gets a little bit... creative? Like, I'll have my work tickets in one place, and then I'll jot down some home repair notes on a sticky note, and maybe I'll email myself a budget reminder, or put something in my phone... or Slack... 

I mean, it's not a complete disaster or anything! I usually find what I need eventually. It's just that sometimes I realize I've spent, you know, a little more time trying to gather up all my notes and figure out what I was supposed to be doing than actually doing the task itself. 

But I think that's pretty normal when you have a lot on your plate, right? I'm sure plenty of people have sticky notes everywhere. I hope that doesn't make me sound completely disorganized, I really do try my best to keep everything straight!

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... interrupting? I really try my hardest not to do that. I would hate for anyone to think I'm being rude or that I don't value what they have to say. 

But... well, I guess sometimes, especially at work, I do get a little ahead of myself. Like, if a teammate is explaining a bug to me, or asking a question, I'll sometimes catch myself answering before they’ve actually finished talking. 

It's mostly just because I want to be helpful and I think I already know what the problem is! But then... sometimes I’m already talking and I realize I completely guessed wrong about where they were going with it. 

I always feel terrible and apologize right away when I do it. I really hope my coworkers know I have good intentions and I'm just trying to keep the momentum going. I'm sure other people get a bit eager like that when they're brainstorming too, right? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of examples. Is that what you meant?

Excessively? Oh, um... no, I really don't think so. I actually pride myself on trying to be a good listener. I would absolutely hate to be the kind of person who just dominates a conversation and doesn't let anyone else get a word in. 

I mean, in our team meetings or daily stand-ups, I usually just give my quick update and then let the next person go. I want everyone to feel included, you know? 

So, no, I wouldn't say I talk more than other people... at least, I really hope my coworkers don't think I do! I always try to be polite and share the floor. Does that answer what you were asking?

Um... trouble waiting my turn? Oh, gosh, I really try not to be one of those impatient people. I know how annoying that can be for everyone else! 

But... well, I guess if I'm being completely honest, there are times when it does get under my skin a little bit. Like, if I'm sitting on hold with the bank for a really long time, I get weirdly irritated. I'll catch myself constantly checking the time, just wanting to get it over with.

I always feel bad afterward because I know it probably comes off as me being impatient, and I really don't want my team to think I don't value what they're saying! Relax? Oh, no, I wouldn't say I find it difficult to relax. I think everyone needs some downtime, right? I'd hate for you to think I'm just constantly wound up or incapable of enjoying a quiet moment.

Stay seated? Um... well, I really try to be respectful and attentive, especially during meetings. I wouldn't want anyone to think I'm not paying attention or that I'm bored with what they're saying! 

But... I guess, sometimes, I do have a little trouble just sitting completely still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive! It's just... well, in meetings, I might be bouncing my knee a bit under the table, or maybe clicking my pen. I also tend to shift around in my chair a fair amount. 

Honestly, I usually don't even realize I'm doing it until someone glances over at me. Then I feel awful and stop right away because I really don't want to be a distraction to the team. It's just a little nervous energy, I think? I really try to keep it under control so it doesn't bother anyone. Is that normal, or is that the kind of thing you're looking for?
